The cited window truncates both ends, and in particular cuts off the pages carrying the compatible/incompatible listings and Macpherson's criticism of Rawlsian equality. The correction is recorded here; ❌ no owner file was edited in this writing pass.
4. ⚠️ **The 2009 India paragraph is reproduced as dated book-period content only.** It is Gauba's statement of the position as he recorded it, and this folder has no standing to state the current position. ❌ It is never updated, never attached to a named judgment, Article or amendment, and never presented as current law.
5. ⚠️ **Roman-numeral section heads render partly as graphics** in the local scan, exactly as in Ch.15. The running all-caps heads recorded in this topic's source audit are sufficient for block identification, and ❌ no further scan work was undertaken.
6. ⚠️ **General Studies and Optional ownership.** None is claimed. Every stem used is a deployment target owned by the Philosophy socio-political files, Polity, Social Justice, Economy or the Essay, and the zero-by-design route count with its no-alias prohibition is restated in full.
7. ⚠️ **Current-linkage limit.** The **nil** recorded in eleven of the twelve pre-teach checklists rests on one bounded topic-level pass carried in this topic's source audit, not on a fresh per-lesson search. The single Lesson 11 anchor is a verbatim reuse of an already-certified repository item and was **not** re-fetched; if it cannot be reproduced exactly as recorded, the correct action is to print **nil** at Lesson 11 as well. Both should be re-run before this edition is relied on as current.
8. ⚠️ **Mechanical-gate reconciliation on the solved workbook.** The topic handoff anticipated recording the PT-18 solved workbook as `checked` in the character of a coverage map. The authoritative validator requires excluded categories to read `not relevant`, and the stricter mechanical rule controls. The coverage-map reading is therefore disclosed in full under *Layered/complete session* and again in the *Solved workbook* row, so that nothing is concealed by the status value.
9. ⚠️ **Scope limit of this edition.** This is a fast-preparation edition for GS-II, GS-IV and the Essay. The declared exclusions in the coverage matrix are deliberate; where a question demands them, the canonical owners and the certified book span are the correct sources.

## Subject boundary observed

This is **Topic 18 of twenty-three**, and it stays within its own syllabus unit. Material it needs but does not own is **named and deferred**, not reconstructed: the rights catalogue and the human-rights / civil-liberties / democratic-rights distinction to **Topic 17**; theories of justice — the original position, the veil of ignorance, the two principles, the difference principle, and entitlement-versus-patterned justice *as a justice debate* — to **Topics 19 and 20**; Gandhian philosophy beyond the printed property close to **Topic 05**; liberalism, Marxism and neoliberalism as ideology surveys to **Topics 03, 04 and 13**; democracy and participation as institutional design to **Topics 22 and 23**; and gender discrimination as a subject to the Philosophy socio-political owner.

❌ **Excluded absolutely, in every lesson:** Article 300-A and any constitutional provision; land-ceiling, land-acquisition, tenancy and compensation statutes; reservation law, the EWS provision and any amendment; any named ongoing or recent litigation on reservation, property, land acquisition or speech; caste-census or survey findings; any inequality index, Gini figure, wealth-share report or poverty estimate; labour-code or minimum-wage status; any farm, tenancy or land-record programme other than the single reproduced NAKSHA anchor; and any named party, government or leader. These are `Polity/`, `Economy/`, `Social-Justice/` and `Current-Affairs/` subjects, and a conceptual session has no standing to characterise them. No constitutional article, amendment, judgment, statute, rule, scheme name, commission, party, budget figure or case citation is asserted anywhere in this edition.
